### Task 1: Spike: derper flags, client verification, visibility grant and build

**Files:**
- Modify: `docs/dforge/specs/2026-10-21-v1-completion-design.md`, section 4.3 only, in two places and no other line:
  - one new bullet `- **Recorded contract (DERP, checked YYYY-MM-DD).**` placed directly before the bullet that starts `- Controller table` (the last bullet of 4.3);
  - the one line `  derper's log showing that it reached tailscaled for client verification);` of the "Binary update" bullet, replaced as Step 3 says.
- Create: `docs/dforge/notes/v1-w5-derp-spike.md`.
- Read only: `go.mod` (the repository's `go` and `toolchain` lines) and Tailscale's public sources and documentation, saved under `$TMPDIR`.
- Test: none; the check script of Step 1 is the test. It lives in `$TMPDIR`, not in the repository.

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: nothing from earlier tasks. Tailscale's public source files at tag `v1.104.1` (and the whois handler at `v1.82.0`), two documentation pages and the Go module proxy, read over the network (only this task and Task 8's build check do; Global Constraints "Host safety"). Nothing is installed; no `derper`, `tailscale` or `tailscaled` command runs, and the derper binaries built in Step 3 are never executed (`go version -m` only reads their build information).
- Produces: one spec bullet and one note that later tasks and the integrator read as fixed facts. Task 7 builds `Flags`, `UnitFile` and its `User=` line from the bullet's Flags and Verify clients entries; Task 8 relies on its Build entry (`CGO_ENABLED=0`, both architectures, binary sizes); Task 13 relies on its Certificate entry (a derper started with `-certmode manual` and an IP `-hostname` on a host without a tailnet); Task 27 and the contract-check gate read its Region node and Visibility grant entries (Contracts "DERP region edits"); Tasks 21, 22 and 25 rely on its Version coupling entry (the pin and hold). The `Divergences` bullet and the note's `## Divergences` heading gate the plan: after wave 1-3 merges, any entry other than `none` stops execution until the integrator amends Contracts and every task that states the old value (Global Constraints "Contract-check gate"). A visibility grant that differs from the provisional one is not a divergence: the gate copies it from the `**Visibility grant:**` line.

**Depends on:** none
**Implementer:** dforge:impl-sonnet-high
**Evaluation:** files 1 - spec 2 - coupling 2 - risk 0 = 5

This task changes no Go code. It settles the facts the plan's Assumptions mark "unverified — Task 1 settles each": whether `-verify-clients` works for a non-root user through tailscaled's socket, whether the version coupling behind the tailscaled pin is backed by an upstream statement, whether derper starts with `-certmode manual` and an IP `-hostname` without a tailnet, the minimal visibility grant, the DERP map node fields, and whether `tailscale.com/cmd/derper@v1.104.1` builds with `CGO_ENABLED=0`. It also records the deviation in the binary update's health check (Assumptions, ruling on the journal reader).

Every divergence, in the spec bullet and in the note, is written in one fixed form, because the integrator acts on it mechanically:

`<Contracts section> — <plan value> → <provider value> — <source URL>`

for example `DERP unit text — User=darkpanel-derp runs derper as a non-root user → derper -verify-clients needs the local API's write permission, which only root holds — https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tailscale/tailscale/v1.104.1/ipn/ipnserver/actor.go` (an illustration of the form, not a finding). The first field is the heading of the Contracts section that states the plan value and is one of `Derper artifact`, `Derper release build`, `DERP server unit`, `DERP unit text`, `DERP internals`, `DERP install, update and renewal effects`, `DERP composites`, `DERP region edits`, `DERP fallback check` or `Harness M5 DERP`; the separators are an em dash with spaces around it and an arrow; the URL is the file that states the provider value. One entry per difference, never two differences in one entry.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the check**

Create `$TMPDIR/check-spike.sh` (after `mkdir -p /root/.cache/w5-tmp/1 && export TMPDIR=/root/.cache/w5-tmp/1 GOTOOLCHAIN=local`) with exactly this content. It verifies that the recorded-contract bullet exists with every required label and verdict phrase, that the grant parses as a JSON object, that each verdict which contradicts the plan has a matching divergence entry, that the `Divergences` bullet is `none` or lists entries in the fixed form and equals the note's `## Divergences` section, that the binary-update health check no longer names derper's log, that no placeholder is left, and that the only files changed are the spec (with hunks only in section 4.3) and the note.

```bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -u
spec=docs/dforge/specs/2026-10-21-v1-completion-design.md
note=docs/dforge/notes/v1-w5-derp-spike.md
fail=0
bad() { echo "FAIL: $*"; fail=1; }

sections='Derper artifact|Derper release build|DERP server unit|DERP unit text|DERP internals|DERP install, update and renewal effects|DERP composites|DERP region edits|DERP fallback check|Harness M5 DERP'
entry_re="^ *- ($sections) — .+ → .+ — https?://[^ ]+\$"
date_re='20[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}'

# The spec's section 4.3 without its heading, and its first and last line numbers.
s43=$(awk 'index($0,"#### 4.3 DERP")==1{f=1;next} index($0,"### 5. Action catalogue")==1{f=0} f' "$spec")
read -r lo hi < <(awk 'index($0,"#### 4.3 DERP")==1{a=NR} index($0,"### 5. Action catalogue")==1&&a{print a, NR-1; exit}' "$spec")

# The recorded-contract bullet: from its first line to the next bullet at column 0.
rc=$(printf '%s\n' "$s43" | awk '/^- \*\*Recorded contract \(DERP, checked /{f=1;print;next} f&&/^- /{f=0} f')
[ -n "$rc" ] || bad "4.3 has no bullet '**Recorded contract (DERP, checked YYYY-MM-DD).**'"
printf '%s\n' "$rc" | head -1 | grep -Eq "checked $date_re\)" || bad "the recorded-contract bullet carries no check date"

# block <label>: the nested bullet with that label and its continuation lines.
block() { printf '%s\n' "$rc" | awk -v l="  - **$1:**" 'index($0,l)==1{f=1;print;next} f&&/^  - /{f=0} f&&/^- /{f=0} f'; }

for l in Sources Flags 'Verify clients' 'Version coupling' Certificate 'Region node' 'Visibility grant' Build 'Health check' Divergences; do
  [ -n "$(block "$l")" ] || bad "the recorded-contract bullet has no nested bullet '**$l:**'"
done

# The divergences of the spec bullet: none, or entries of the fixed form.
div_block=$(block Divergences)
spec_entries=$(printf '%s\n' "$div_block" | tail -n +2 | grep -E '^ +- ' | sed -E 's/^ +- //' | sort)
if printf '%s\n' "$div_block" | head -1 | grep -Eq '\*\*Divergences:\*\* *none\.? *$'; then
  [ -z "$spec_entries" ] || bad "Divergences says none and also lists entries"
  spec_none=1
else
  spec_none=0
  [ -n "$spec_entries" ] || bad "Divergences is neither none nor followed by entries"
  printf '%s\n' "$div_block" | tail -n +2 | grep -Ev '^$' | grep -Evq "$entry_re" && bad "a Divergences line is not '<Contracts section> — <plan value> → <provider value> — <source URL>' with a section of the plan's Contracts"
fi
needs_divergence() { [ "$spec_none" -eq 0 ] || bad "$1, so Divergences must list an entry, not none"; }

b=$(block Flags)
printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Matches the plan: yes' || { printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Matches the plan: no' && needs_divergence "Flags do not match the plan" || bad "Flags lacks 'Matches the plan: yes' or 'Matches the plan: no'"; }

b=$(block 'Verify clients')
if printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Conclusion: non-root works'; then :
elif printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Conclusion: non-root does not work'; then
  needs_divergence "-verify-clients does not work for a non-root user"
  printf '%s\n' "$spec_entries" | grep -Fq 'DERP unit text — ' || bad "-verify-clients needs root, but Divergences has no 'DERP unit text — ' entry"
else bad "Verify clients lacks 'Conclusion: non-root works' or 'Conclusion: non-root does not work'"; fi
printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Fail-open: ' || bad "Verify clients lacks a 'Fail-open: ' statement"

b=$(block 'Version coupling')
if printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Eq 'Outcome: (cited|relaxed|unbacked)'; then
  if printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Outcome: relaxed'; then
    needs_divergence "the version coupling is relaxed"
    printf '%s\n' "$spec_entries" | grep -Fq 'DERP composites — ' || bad "the coupling is relaxed, but Divergences has no 'DERP composites — ' entry"
  fi
  if ! printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Outcome: unbacked'; then
    printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Eq 'https?://[^ ]+' || bad "Version coupling cites no URL"
  fi
else bad "Version coupling lacks 'Outcome: cited', 'Outcome: relaxed' or 'Outcome: unbacked'"; fi

b=$(block Certificate)
printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Eq 'Starts without a tailnet: (yes|no)' || bad "Certificate lacks 'Starts without a tailnet: yes' or 'Starts without a tailnet: no'"
if printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Starts without a tailnet: no'; then
  needs_divergence "derper does not start without a tailnet"
  printf '%s\n' "$spec_entries" | grep -Fq 'Harness M5 DERP — ' || bad "derper needs a tailnet, but Divergences has no 'Harness M5 DERP — ' entry"
fi
printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Matches the plan: yes' || { printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Matches the plan: no' && needs_divergence "the certificate behaviour does not match the plan" || bad "Certificate lacks 'Matches the plan: yes' or 'Matches the plan: no'"; }

b=$(block 'Region node')
printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Matches the plan: yes' || { printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Matches the plan: no' && needs_divergence "the region node does not match the plan" || bad "Region node lacks 'Matches the plan: yes' or 'Matches the plan: no'"; }

b=$(block 'Visibility grant')
grant=$(printf '%s\n' "$b" | head -1 | grep -o '`{[^`]*}`' | head -1 | tr -d '`' | sed 's/<tag>/tag:derp/g')
if [ -z "$grant" ]; then bad "Visibility grant has no grant as a JSON object in backticks on its first line"
else
  printf '%s\n' "$grant" | jq -e '(keys - ["src","dst","ip"]) == [] and (.src|type=="array" and length>0) and (.dst|type=="array" and index("tag:derp")!=null) and (.ip|type=="array" and length>0)' >/dev/null 2>&1 \
    || bad "the grant is not an object with non-empty src and ip arrays and a dst array holding the placeholder <tag>: $grant"
fi
printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Eq 'https?://[^ ]+|not documented' || bad "Visibility grant names no source URL and does not say 'not documented'"

b=$(block Build)
if printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Eq 'Built: CGO_ENABLED=0 .*go1\.[0-9]+.*amd64 [0-9]+ bytes.*arm64 [0-9]+ bytes'; then :
elif printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'Built: failed'; then
  needs_divergence "the derper build failed"
  printf '%s\n' "$spec_entries" | grep -Fq 'Derper release build — ' || bad "the build failed, but Divergences has no 'Derper release build — ' entry"
else bad "Build lacks 'Built: CGO_ENABLED=0 ... go1.N ... amd64 N bytes ... arm64 N bytes' or 'Built: failed'"; fi
for size in $(printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -oE '(amd64|arm64) [0-9]+ bytes' | awk '{print $2}'); do
  if [ "$size" -gt 134217728 ]; then
    needs_divergence "a derper binary is over 128 MiB"
    printf '%s\n' "$spec_entries" | grep -Fq 'Derper artifact — ' || bad "a derper binary is over 128 MiB, but Divergences has no 'Derper artifact — ' entry"
  fi
done

b=$(block 'Health check')
printf '%s\n' "$b" | grep -Fq 'tailscaled answers `Status`' || bad "Health check does not record the replacement 'tailscaled answers \`Status\`'"
printf '%s\n' "$s43" | grep -Fq "derper's log showing" && bad "4.3 still names derper's log in the binary-update health check"

# The note repeats the divergences.
if [ ! -f "$note" ]; then bad "$note does not exist"
else
  for h in '## Sources' '## Findings' '## Divergences'; do grep -Fxq "$h" "$note" || bad "$note lacks the heading '$h'"; done
  grep -Eq "^Checked: $date_re\$" "$note" || bad "$note lacks a line 'Checked: YYYY-MM-DD'"
  note_div=$(awk '/^## Divergences$/{f=1;next} /^## /{f=0} f&&NF' "$note")
  if [ "$note_div" = "none" ]; then
    [ "$spec_none" -eq 1 ] || bad "the note says none but the spec bullet lists entries"
  else
    [ "$spec_none" -eq 0 ] || bad "the note lists entries but the spec bullet says none"
    printf '%s\n' "$note_div" | grep -Ev "^- ($sections) — .+ → .+ — https?://[^ ]+\$" && bad "a divergence line of the note is not in the fixed form"
    [ "$(printf '%s\n' "$note_div" | sed -E 's/^- //' | sort)" = "$spec_entries" ] || bad "the note's divergences differ from the spec bullet's"
  fi
fi

printf '%s\n%s\n' "$s43" "$(cat "$note" 2>/dev/null)" | grep -En 'TBD|TODO|\?\?\?' && bad "a placeholder is left in 4.3 or the note"

stray=$(git diff -U0 HEAD -- "$spec" | grep -E '^@@' | while read -r _ _ plus _; do
  start=${plus#+}; start=${start%%,*}; len=1
  case $plus in *,*) len=${plus#*,};; esac
  end=$((start + len - 1)); [ "$len" -eq 0 ] && end=$start
  if [ "$start" -ge "$lo" ] && [ "$end" -le "$hi" ]; then continue; fi
  echo "hunk +$start,$len"
done)
[ -z "$stray" ] || bad "the spec changed outside section 4.3: $stray"

others=$(git status --porcelain | grep -v -e " $spec\$" -e " $note\$" || true)
[ -z "$others" ] || bad "files other than the spec and the note changed: $others"

[ "$fail" -eq 0 ] && echo "spike check OK"
exit "$fail"
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the check to verify it fails**

Run, from the repository root: `bash $TMPDIR/check-spike.sh`
Expected: exit status 1 and 26 `FAIL:` lines: the bullet and its check date reported missing, ten nested labels reported missing, "Divergences is neither none nor followed by entries", one missing-verdict line each for Flags, Verify clients (two lines: the conclusion and the fail-open statement), Version coupling, Certificate (two lines), Region node, Visibility grant (two lines), Build and Health check, "4.3 still names derper's log in the binary-update health check", and "docs/dforge/notes/v1-w5-derp-spike.md does not exist". No "changed outside" line and no "files other than" line, because nothing is edited yet.

- [ ] **Step 3: Read the sources and record the result**

Network rules. Use only unauthenticated `curl -sSL --max-time 60` GETs of the URLs below and the module proxy through `go get`. Send no credential and make no authenticated request. Save each source in its own new empty directory under `$TMPDIR/docs/` (the files of one Tailscale tag together in `ts-<tag>/`, the documentation pages in `kb/`), keep every script you write in `$TMPDIR/scripts/`, and read files with `rg` and `sed -n`. If a source cannot be fetched, stop and report BLOCKED with the URL; never fill a fact in from memory. State only what a source says; where a source is silent write `not documented`, never a guess.

Download the Tailscale source files for `v1.104.1`, and the whois handler for `v1.82.0` (the oldest tailscaled the spec supports, Versions table). A path that answers 404 at a tag is not at that path there: delete the saved error page, list the directory with `curl -sSL "https://api.github.com/repos/tailscale/tailscale/contents/<dir>?ref=<tag>"` (an unauthenticated read) and download the file that holds the symbol you need (for example the DERP server moved from `derp/derp_server.go` to a `derp/derpserver` package, and `client/local` was `client/tailscale` in older tags).

```bash
tag=v1.104.1
paths="cmd/derper/derper.go cmd/derper/cert.go derp/derp_server.go tailcfg/derpmap.go net/tlsdial/tlsdial.go ipn/localapi/localapi.go ipn/ipnserver/actor.go safesocket/unixsocket.go client/local/local.go go.mod"
mkdir -p "$TMPDIR/docs/ts-$tag" "$TMPDIR/docs/ts-v1.82.0" "$TMPDIR/docs/kb"
for p in $paths; do
  curl -sSL --max-time 60 -o "$TMPDIR/docs/ts-$tag/$(echo "$p" | tr / _)" -w "$tag $p %{http_code}\n" "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tailscale/tailscale/$tag/$p"
done
curl -sSL --max-time 60 -o "$TMPDIR/docs/ts-v1.82.0/ipn_localapi_localapi.go" -w "v1.82.0 ipn/localapi/localapi.go %{http_code}\n" "https://raw.githubusercontent.com/tailscale/tailscale/v1.82.0/ipn/localapi/localapi.go"
for page in 1118/custom-derp-servers 1337/policy-syntax; do
  f="$TMPDIR/docs/kb/$(echo "$page" | tr / _)"
  curl -sSL --max-time 60 -o "$f.html" -w "$page %{http_code}\n" "https://tailscale.com/kb/$page"
  sed -e 's/<[^>]*>/ /g' "$f.html" | tr -s ' \t' ' ' > "$f.txt"
done
```

Build derper into `$TMPDIR` the way `releasetool derper` will (Contracts "Derper release build"). The environment variables are set inside the subshell only, so the gates of Step 4 keep the default caches. Do not run the binaries.

```bash
( export GOMODCACHE="$TMPDIR/gomod" GOCACHE="$TMPDIR/gocache" GOFLAGS=-modcacherw GOTOOLCHAIN=local
  mkdir -p "$TMPDIR/derperbuild/out"
  timeout 120 go -C "$TMPDIR/derperbuild" mod init derperbuild
  timeout 600 go -C "$TMPDIR/derperbuild" get tailscale.com/cmd/derper@v1.104.1
  for arch in amd64 arm64; do
    CGO_ENABLED=0 GOOS=linux GOARCH=$arch timeout 900 go -C "$TMPDIR/derperbuild" build -trimpath -ldflags "-s -w" \
      -o "$TMPDIR/derperbuild/out/derper-v1.104.1-linux-$arch" tailscale.com/cmd/derper
  done
  ls -l "$TMPDIR/derperbuild/out"
  go version -m "$TMPDIR/derperbuild/out/derper-v1.104.1-linux-amd64" | head -20
  sed -n 1,6p "$GOMODCACHE/cache/download/tailscale.com/@v/v1.104.1.mod" )
```

Edit the spec and write the note as follows. Use the absolute date you ran the check as `YYYY-MM-DD`. The new bullet is factual; each nested bullet is `  - **Label:** ...` with continuation lines indented four spaces, and holds at most about ten lines. Divergence entries are nested bullets (`    - <entry>`) under the `**Divergences:**` label, or the single word `none` on that label's line.

1. **The bullet.** Add `- **Recorded contract (DERP, checked YYYY-MM-DD).**` with exactly these nested bullets, in this order. Each verdict phrase in quotes is parsed by the check and must appear verbatim:
   - `**Sources:**` each URL read, as `tag path` for source files and the full URL for documentation pages.
   - `**Flags:**` from `cmd/derper/derper.go` at `v1.104.1`: that each of `-c`, `-a`, `-http-port`, `-stun`, `-stun-port`, `-hostname`, `-certmode`, `-certdir` and `-verify-clients` exists under that name, with its type (the booleans take no value) and default; the values `-certmode` accepts; that `-certmode letsencrypt` serves the ACME challenge on the `-http-port` listener; the path, method and status of the `/derp/probe` handler and that it is served over TLS on the `-a` listener; the names `-verify-client-url` and `-verify-client-url-fail-open` (neither is used); who creates the `-c` key file and with which mode. Then `Matches the plan: yes` or `Matches the plan: no`, measured against the argument vector of Contracts "DERP server unit" (`Flags`).
   - `**Verify clients:**` from `cmd/derper/derper.go`, the DERP server source, `client/local/local.go`, `ipn/localapi/localapi.go`, `ipn/ipnserver/actor.go` and `safesocket/unixsocket.go`: (a) the local API call derper makes to check a client and the client code it uses; (b) the socket path derper uses by default and whether a flag changes it; (c) the permission the handler demands (`PermitRead`, `PermitWrite` or `PermitCert`) and what the local API grants a connecting user that is neither root nor the operator, with the socket file's mode when tailscaled listens; (d) what derper does when tailscaled does not answer, and whether derper starts at all without tailscaled when `-verify-clients` is set; (e) which check `-verify-client-url-fail-open` governs. End with `Conclusion: non-root works` or `Conclusion: non-root does not work` (read from the source, not run), and `Fail-open: ` followed by one sentence stating whether any flag makes `-verify-clients` fail open.
   - `**Version coupling:**` search the custom-DERP page and the source comments for a statement that `-verify-clients` needs derper and tailscaled at the same version. Then `Outcome: cited` (a statement exists: quote it, at most three lines, with its URL), `Outcome: relaxed` (a statement says that derper works with an older or newer tailscaled: quote it with its URL) or `Outcome: unbacked` (no statement either way: the pin and hold stay, and the bullet says that the assumption is unbacked; add as supporting facts, not proof, whether the whois-by-node-key handler is the same at `v1.82.0` and `v1.104.1`).
   - `**Certificate:**` from `cmd/derper/cert.go` and `net/tlsdial/tlsdial.go`: with `-certmode manual` and an IP `-hostname`, the file names derper reads and writes in `-certdir` (the plan expects `<ip>.crt` and `<ip>.key`), the condition under which it generates a self-signed certificate, its validity period and subject alternative names, whether the files are read only at start, the modes of the files it writes, and whether the certificate directory must exist beforehand; the shape of the `CertName` pin (`sha256-raw:` followed by the hex SHA-256 of the leaf certificate's DER) and the `NotBefore` and `NotAfter` checks the client makes. Then `Starts without a tailnet: yes` or `Starts without a tailnet: no` (derper without `-verify-clients` reaching neither tailscaled nor Tailscale's control plane at start, from the source), and `Matches the plan: yes` or `Matches the plan: no`, measured against spec 4.3 "Flags" (the `ip` bullet) and Contracts "DERP install, update and renewal effects".
   - `**Region node:**` from `tailcfg/derpmap.go` and `net/tlsdial/tlsdial.go` at `v1.104.1`, and the policy-syntax page: the JSON name of each `DERPNode` field (`Name`, `RegionID`, `HostName`, `CertName`, `IPv4`, `IPv6`, `STUNPort`, `STUNOnly`, `DERPPort`) and `DERPRegion` field (`RegionID`, `RegionCode`, `RegionName`, `Nodes`) with its documented meaning; the value that means "none" for `IPv4` and `IPv6`; what `STUNPort` and `DERPPort` mean at 0; the rule for `Name`; the placement of `Regions` and `OmitDefaultRegions` under `derpMap` in the policy file. Then `Matches the plan: yes` or `Matches the plan: no`, measured against Contracts "DERP region edits" (the region object `{"RegionID", "RegionCode", "RegionName", "Nodes": [{"Name": "<id>a", "RegionID", "HostName", "DERPPort": 443, "STUNPort": 3478}]}`, and in `ip` mode `IPv4` or `IPv6` plus `CertName`).
   - `**Visibility grant:**` the minimal tailnet policy grant that makes every client node visible to the DERP host's tailscaled, as the custom-DERP page or the policy-syntax page documents it. The first line holds the grant as a JSON object in backticks, with the literal text `<tag>` as the destination tag, then the source URL or `not documented; the provisional grant stands`. The provisional grant, to keep when the pages are silent, is `{"src": ["*"], "dst": ["<tag>"], "ip": ["tcp:443"]}`. Add on the following lines why the grant is minimal and what the page says about visibility.
   - `**Build:**` the result of the build above on one line: `Built: CGO_ENABLED=0 go1.N.N, amd64 <bytes> bytes, arm64 <bytes> bytes` (the toolchain from `go version`, the sizes from `ls -l`), then that `go version -m` reports `CGO_ENABLED=0` and `tailscale.com v1.104.1`, and the `go` directive of tailscale.com v1.104.1's `go.mod` against this repository's `go 1.27` and `toolchain go1.27.3`. If the build fails, write `Built: failed` and the first error line. A binary above 134217728 bytes (the 128 MiB limit of `artifact.Fetch`) needs a `Derper artifact` divergence.
   - `**Health check:**` exactly this sentence: `The binary update's health check uses "tailscaled answers `Status` on its socket" in place of "derper's log showing that it reached tailscaled": the exec allowlist has no command that reads a journal.`
   - `**Divergences:**` each place where the sources differ from the plan's Contracts, in the fixed form, one nested bullet each, or `none` on the label's line. A `Matches the plan: no`, a `Conclusion: non-root does not work`, a `Starts without a tailnet: no`, an `Outcome: relaxed`, a failed build and an oversized binary each need an entry, in the Contracts section the check expects: `DERP unit text` for a non-root conclusion, `DERP composites` for a relaxed coupling, `Harness M5 DERP` for a tailnet-dependent start, `Derper release build` for a failed build, `Derper artifact` for an oversized binary. A different visibility grant is not an entry.

```go
		{"derperVersion with two numbers", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, `"derperVersion":"v1.104"`, mfArtifacts), "derperVersion"},
		{"derperVersion of the wrong type", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, `"derperVersion":5`, mfArtifacts), "derperVersion"},

		{"arm64 missing", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, mfDerper, arts(amd)), "arm64"},
		{"amd64 missing", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, mfDerper, arts(arm)), "amd64"},
		{"no artifacts at all", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, mfDerper, arts("")), "amd64"},
		{"an upper-case hash", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, mfDerper, arts(`"amd64":"`+strings.ToUpper(mfHashAMD)+`",`+arm)), "amd64"},
		{"a short hash", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, mfDerper, arts(amd+`,"arm64":"`+mfHashARM[:63]+`"`)), "arm64"},
		{"a hash that is not a string", mfDoc(mfDarkpanel, mfDerper, arts(amd+`,"arm64":7`)), "artifacts"},
	}

			}

			}
		})

}

func TestMfEncodeValidatesTheManifest(t *testing.T) {
	tests := []struct {

		mutate func(*Manifest)
		want   string
	}{
		{"empty darkpanel version", func(m *Manifest) { m.DarkpanelVersion = "" }, "darkpanelVersion"},
		{"darkpanel version with a v", func(m *Manifest) { m.DarkpanelVersion = "v1.4.0" }, "darkpanelVersion"},
		{"derper version without a v", func(m *Manifest) { m.DerperVersion = "1.104.1" }, "derperVersion"},
		{"missing arch", func(m *Manifest) { delete(m.Artifacts, "arm64") }, "arm64"},
		{"extra arch", func(m *Manifest) { m.Artifacts["s390x"] = mfHashAMD }, "s390x"},
		{"bad hash", func(m *Manifest) { m.Artifacts["amd64"] = "xyz" }, "amd64"},
		{"nil artifacts", func(m *Manifest) { m.Artifacts = nil }, "amd64"},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			m := mfManifest()
			tt.mutate(&m)
			b, err := m.Encode()
			if err == nil {
				t.Fatalf("Encode accepted %+v and wrote %s", m, b)
			}
			if b != nil || !strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), "artifact:") || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tt.want) {
				t.Errorf("Encode = %q, %v, want no bytes and an \"artifact:\" error mentioning %q", b, err, tt.want)
			}
		})
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

1. Create `release/derper.version` holding the single line `v1.104.1` and a trailing newline, the version the harness tailscaled runs (Contracts "Derper artifact"):

   ```bash
   printf 'v1.104.1\n' > release/derper.version
   ```

2. Create `internal/modules/derp/artifact/manifest.go` with the package comment ("Package artifact is the derper release artifact: its version, its derper.json manifest and its verified fetch."), the exported identifiers exactly as Contracts "Derper artifact" gives them (each with its doc comment): the constants `DerperVersion = "v1.104.1"` and `ManifestName = "derper.json"`, the variable `Arches = []string{"amd64", "arm64"}`, `BinaryName`, `PinVersion`, `Manifest` with the three JSON names `darkpanelVersion`, `derperVersion`, `artifacts` in that field order, `ParseManifest` and `Manifest.Encode`.
3. `BinaryName(version, arch)` is `"derper-" + version + "-linux-" + arch` and validates nothing. `PinVersion()` is `DerperVersion` without its leading "v".
4. One unexported validation method on `Manifest` serves both `ParseManifest` and `Encode`. It refuses, in this order, with an error that starts `artifact:` and names the offending member: a `DarkpanelVersion` that `update.ParseVersion` does not accept (this also refuses a leading "v" and the empty string; `0.0.0` and prereleases such as `1.5.0-beta.1` are accepted); a `DerperVersion` that does not match `^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$`; a key of `Artifacts` that is not in `Arches` (the error names the key); an arch of `Arches` that `Artifacts` lacks (names the arch; a nil map lacks `amd64` first); a hash that does not match `^[0-9a-f]{64}$` (names the arch).
5. `ParseManifest` decodes strictly without `DisallowUnknownFields`, because that cannot tell a missing member from a zero one: it decodes the document into a `map[string]json.RawMessage` (an error starting `artifact: read derper.json:` for input that is not a JSON object), refuses data after the first value (the error contains "trailing"), refuses a member other than the three (the error names it), refuses a missing member (the error names it, `artifacts` included when it is `null`), decodes each member into its field (an error that names the member, so `"derperVersion": 5` and `"arm64": 7` mention `derperVersion` and `artifacts`), and then runs the validation of item 4. It accepts the members in any order and any whitespace.
6. `Encode` runs the validation, then answers `json.MarshalIndent(m, "", "  ")` followed by one newline; on a validation error it answers nil bytes and the error. Map keys come out sorted, as `encoding/json` sorts them.

### Task 3: update: verified release assets

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/update/asset.go`
- Modify: `internal/update/download.go` (`Download`: the checksums.txt fetch and signature check, from the line `base := strings.TrimSuffix(baseURL, "/") + "/v" + rel.Version + "/"` through the `VerifyChecksums` call, move into one unexported helper)
- Test: `internal/update/asset_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: in package `update`, `Release`, `ParseVersion`, `VerifyChecksums`, `checksumFor`, `fetchBytes`, `errNotServed` and the limits `maxChecksumsBytes` and `maxSignatureBytes` (`download.go`); `trustedkeys.Key`; the error codes `errcode.UpdateNotFound` and `errcode.UpdateSignatureInvalid`; the test helpers `newReleaseServer`, `newTestSigner`, `testSigner.sign`, `releaseComment` and `checksumLine` (`release_test.go`).
- Produces: Contracts "Release asset download (from Task 3)": `DownloadAsset`. Task 9 calls it for `derper.json` and for the derper binary. `Download` keeps its signature and behaviour; `download_test.go` stays unchanged and guards the refactor.

**Depends on:** none
**Implementer:** dforge:impl-opus-high
**Evaluation:** files 1 - spec 2 - coupling 2 - risk 2 = 7

`DownloadAsset` is `Download` for a file that is not the tarball: the same signed `checksums.txt`, the same channel binding, then one named asset held in memory. The one signed-checksums path is shared, so an asset can never be accepted under weaker checks than the tarball. The code names the last parameter `limit`, not `max` as the Contracts text writes it: revive's `redefines-builtin-id` refuses `max` and callers pass it by position, so the name is not part of the contract.

Create `internal/update/asset_test.go`:

```go
package update

import (
	"context"
	"errors"
	"net/http"
	"net/http/httptest"
	"sort"
	"strings"
	"sync/atomic"
	"testing"

	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/errcode"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/update/trustedkeys"
)

// asPublish publishes assets under v<version>/ with a checksums.txt that lists
// exactly them, signed for channel by every signer.
func asPublish(srv *releaseServer, version, channel string, assets map[string][]byte, signers ...testSigner) {
	dir := "v" + version + "/"
	names := make([]string, 0, len(assets))
	for name := range assets {
		names = append(names, name)
	}
	sort.Strings(names)
	var sums strings.Builder
	for _, name := range names {
		srv.put(dir+name, assets[name])
		sums.WriteString(checksumLine(assets[name], name))
	}
	srv.put(dir+"checksums.txt", []byte(sums.String()))
	for _, s := range signers {
		srv.put(dir+"checksums.txt."+s.Key.ID+".minisig", s.sign([]byte(sums.String()), releaseComment(version, channel)))
	}
}

func TestAsDownloadAssetReturnsTheVerifiedAsset(t *testing.T) {
	signer, second := newTestSigner(t), newTestSigner(t)
	longName := strings.Repeat("a", 128)
	assets := map[string][]byte{
		"derper.json":                 []byte(`{"derperVersion":"v1.104.1"}`),
		"derper-v1.104.1-linux-arm64": []byte("arm64 binary"),
		"A_b.c-d":                     []byte("odd characters"),
		"a":                           []byte("one character"),
		longName:                      []byte("longest name"),
	}
	srv := newReleaseServer(t)
	asPublish(srv, "1.3.0", "stable", assets, signer)
	keys := []trustedkeys.Key{second.Key, signer.Key}
	for name, want := range assets {
		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got, err := DownloadAsset(context.Background(), srv.client(), srv.URL+"/", keys,
				Release{Version: "1.3.0", Channel: "stable"}, name, int64(len(want)))
			if err != nil {
				t.Fatal(err)
			}
			if string(got) != string(want) {
				t.Errorf("DownloadAsset(%s) = %q, want %q", name, got, want)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestAsDownloadAssetRefuses(t *testing.T) {
	signer := newTestSigner(t)
	keys := []trustedkeys.Key{signer.Key}
	body := []byte("0123456789")
	stable := Release{Version: "1.3.0", Channel: "stable"}
	tests := []struct {
		name    string
		rel     Release
		asset   string
		max     int64
		tamper  func(srv *releaseServer)
		code    errcode.Code
		message string
	}{
		{name: "release not published", rel: Release{Version: "1.9.0", Channel: "stable"}, asset: "derper.json", max: 64,
			code: errcode.UpdateNotFound},
		{name: "version that is not a version", rel: Release{Version: "nope", Channel: "stable"}, asset: "derper.json", max: 64,
			message: "semantic version"},
		{name: "signature file missing", rel: stable, asset: "derper.json", max: 64, tamper: func(srv *releaseServer) {
			srv.remove("v1.3.0/checksums.txt." + signer.Key.ID + ".minisig")
		}, code: errcode.UpdateSignatureInvalid},
		{name: "older checksums.txt replayed", rel: stable, asset: "derper.json", max: 64, tamper: func(srv *releaseServer) {
			srv.put("v1.3.0/checksums.txt", srv.get("v1.2.0/checksums.txt"))
			srv.put("v1.3.0/checksums.txt."+signer.Key.ID+".minisig", srv.get("v1.2.0/checksums.txt."+signer.Key.ID+".minisig"))
		}, code: errcode.UpdateSignatureInvalid},
		{name: "signed for beta, listed as stable", rel: Release{Version: "1.4.0-beta.1", Channel: "stable"}, asset: "derper.json", max: 64,
			code: errcode.UpdateSignatureInvalid},
		{name: "checksums.txt over its limit", rel: stable, asset: "derper.json", max: 64, tamper: func(srv *releaseServer) {
			srv.put("v1.3.0/checksums.txt", []byte(strings.Repeat("#", 64<<10+1)))
		}, message: "bytes"},
		{name: "asset not listed", rel: stable, asset: "missing.json", max: 64,
			code: errcode.UpdateNotFound, message: "lists no missing.json"},
		{name: "asset listed but not served", rel: stable, asset: "derper.json", max: 64, tamper: func(srv *releaseServer) {
			srv.remove("v1.3.0/derper.json")
		}, code: errcode.UpdateNotFound},
		{name: "asset does not match its line", rel: stable, asset: "derper.json", max: 64, tamper: func(srv *releaseServer) {
			srv.put("v1.3.0/derper.json", []byte("0123456780"))
		}, code: errcode.UpdateSignatureInvalid, message: "derper.json does not match the signed checksums.txt"},
		{name: "asset over the limit", rel: stable, asset: "derper.json", max: int64(len(body)) - 1, message: "9 bytes"},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		t.Run(tt.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			srv := newReleaseServer(t)
			asPublish(srv, "1.2.0", "stable", map[string][]byte{"derper.json": body}, signer)
			asPublish(srv, "1.3.0", "stable", map[string][]byte{"derper.json": body}, signer)
			asPublish(srv, "1.4.0-beta.1", "beta", map[string][]byte{"derper.json": body}, signer)
			if tt.tamper != nil {
				tt.tamper(srv)
			}
			got, err := DownloadAsset(context.Background(), srv.client(), srv.URL, keys, tt.rel, tt.asset, tt.max)
			var ce *errcode.Error
			switch {
			case err == nil:
				t.Fatalf("DownloadAsset accepted the asset and returned %q", got)
			case tt.code != "" && (!errors.As(err, &ce) || ce.Code != tt.code):
				t.Fatalf("DownloadAsset = %v, want %s", err, tt.code)
			case tt.message != "" && !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tt.message):
				t.Fatalf("DownloadAsset = %v, want an error mentioning %q", err, tt.message)
			}
			if got != nil {
				t.Errorf("a refused asset returned %q", got)
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestAsDownloadAssetRefusesNamesThatAreNotAssetNamesBeforeAnyRequest(t *testing.T) {
	var hits atomic.Int64
	srv := httptest.NewServer(http.HandlerFunc(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
		hits.Add(1)
		http.NotFound(w, r)
	}))
	t.Cleanup(srv.Close)
	for _, name := range []string{
		"", "../checksums.txt", "a/b", "/etc/passwd", ".hidden", "-flag", "_x", "a b", "x?y", "x#y", "x\ny", "café", "a:b",
		strings.Repeat("a", 129),
	} {
		_, err := DownloadAsset(context.Background(), srv.Client(), srv.URL, nil, Release{Version: "1.3.0", Channel: "stable"}, name, 64)
		if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), "is not an asset name") {
			t.Errorf("DownloadAsset(%q) = %v, want an error saying it is not an asset name", name, err)
		}
	}
	if n := hits.Load(); n != 0 {
		t.Errorf("%d requests reached the server for names that are not asset names, want none", n)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

1. In `download.go`, add the unexported helper `verifiedChecksums(ctx context.Context, client *http.Client, base string, keys []trustedkeys.Key, rel Release) ([]byte, error)` holding, unchanged, what `Download` does from the `checksums.txt` fetch through the `VerifyChecksums` call: fetch `base+"checksums.txt"` (at most `maxChecksumsBytes`; `errNotServed` becomes `errcode.UpdateNotFound` "release <version> is not published"), fetch `base+"checksums.txt."+k.ID+".minisig"` for every key (at most `maxSignatureBytes`; a missing signature is skipped, the first other fetch error is remembered), return the context's error when it ended (a cancelled download is not a bad signature), run `VerifyChecksums(checksums, sigs, keys, rel.Version, rel.Channel)` and, when it fails, return the remembered fetch error if there is one and the verification error otherwise; on success return the checksums. `Download` calls the helper after its own version and architecture checks and continues as before with the tarball line. No message, code or limit of `Download` changes.
2. Create `asset.go` with `DownloadAsset` as Contracts "Release asset download" states, parameter named `limit`, and a package-level compiled pattern `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}$` for asset names. In this order: `ParseVersion(rel.Version)` (its error is returned, as `Download` returns it); the name check, before any request, with the error `update: <name quoted with %q> is not an asset name`; the base `strings.TrimSuffix(baseURL, "/") + "/v" + rel.Version + "/"`; `verifiedChecksums`; `checksumFor(checksums, name)` (a name that `checksums.txt` does not list is `update.not_found` from `checksumFor`); `fetchBytes(ctx, client, base+name, limit)` (`errNotServed` becomes `errcode.UpdateNotFound` "release <version> has no <name>"; an asset over `limit` is `fetchBytes`'s error "update: download <url>: over <limit> bytes", which names the limit; an asset of exactly `limit` bytes is accepted); the asset's SHA-256 against the listed hash (a mismatch is `errcode.UpdateSignatureInvalid` "<name> does not match the signed checksums.txt"). Every error answers a nil slice. Every URL is built here from `baseURL`; the name never contributes a path separator, a query or a fragment because the pattern refuses them.
3. Run `gofmt -l internal/update` and fix what it lists. A doc comment on `DownloadAsset`; comments only for a non-obvious why.

The two actions keep their output byte for byte: their handlers now call the same methods the exported functions call, so a controller-side module reads exactly what `tailnet.policy.get` and `tailnet.device.list` answer. The exported functions use one package-level `tn` built by `productionTN()`, so every caller shares one token cache. The two pinned behaviours (a `set` and a `delete` at `/derpMap/Regions/900`, where the member name is digits, and an empty `edits` list) are expected to hold already but are unverified (Assumptions), so their tests are expected to pass at Step 2, and Step 3 item 4 applies if one does not; they stay as regression tests because Task 27's `derp.region.edits` and Tasks 21 to 23 depend on them.

```go
package tailnet

import (
	"context"
	"encoding/json"

	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/errcode"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/modules/tailnet/fake"

const exPolicy = `// Darkpanel tailnet policy.
{
	// who may reach what
	"grants": [
		{"src": ["*"], "dst": ["*"], "ip": ["*"]},
	],
}
`

func TestExReadPolicyAnswersWhatTheActionAnswers(t *testing.T) {
	srv := fake.New(t)
	etag := srv.SetPolicy(exPolicy)
	h := newTNHost(t)
	acConnect(t, h, srv)
	n := tnWith(srv)
	got, err := n.readPolicy(context.Background(), h.env(""))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if want := (Policy{Text: exPolicy, ETag: etag}); got != want {
		t.Errorf("readPolicy = %+v, want the stored text with its comments and ETag %s", got, etag)
	}
	raw, err := n.policyGet().Handler.Read(context.Background(), h.env(""), json.RawMessage(`{}`))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var viaAction Policy
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &viaAction); err != nil || viaAction != got {
		t.Errorf("tailnet.policy.get answered %+v (%v), want %+v", viaAction, err, got)
	}
}

func TestExListDevicesAnswersWhatTheActionAnswers(t *testing.T) {
	srv := fake.New(t)

		Tags: []string{"tag:server"}, Authorized: true})

		AdvertisedRoutes: []string{"::/0", "0.0.0.0/0"}})
	bare := srv.AddDevice(fake.Device{Name: "bare.example.ts.net", Hostname: "bare"})
	n := tnWith(srv)
	got, err := n.listDevices(context.Background(), h.env(""))
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var ids []string
	for _, d := range got {
		ids = append(ids, d.ID)
	}
	if want := []string{bare, gw, web}; !slices.Equal(ids, want) {
		t.Fatalf("listDevices = %v, want %v (by name)", ids, want)
	}
	if b := got[0]; b.Addresses == nil || b.Tags == nil || b.AdvertisedRoutes == nil || b.EnabledRoutes == nil {
		t.Errorf("bare device %+v, want empty lists, never nil", b)
	}
	if !got[1].ExitNode || got[1].ExitNodeEnabled {
		t.Errorf("gateway %+v, want an exit node that is not approved", got[1])

}

func TestExReadsNeedAConnectedAccount(t *testing.T) {

	_, errDevices := n.listDevices(ctx, h.env(""))
	_, errSharedPolicy := ReadPolicy(ctx, h.env(""))
	_, errSharedDevices := ListDevices(ctx, h.env(""))
	for name, err := range map[string]error{
		"readPolicy": errPolicy, "listDevices": errDevices, "ReadPolicy": errSharedPolicy, "ListDevices": errSharedDevices,
	} {

	}
}

}
```

Append to `internal/modules/tailnet/policy_patch_test.go` these two tests, and add `"io"`, `"log/slog"`, `"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/coretest"`, `"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/lifecycle"` and `"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/schema"` to its import block (gofmt-sorted; `core`, `errcode`, `fake`, `http`, `json` and `context` are already imported):

```go
// TestExPatchWithNoEditsIsANoOp: an empty edits list, which a composite's
// edits step answers when the policy already holds the result, plans an empty
// diff and applies without a provider validation or write.
func TestExPatchWithNoEditsIsANoOp(t *testing.T) {
	w := ppNew(t, ppPolicy)
	ctx := context.Background()
	in := ppInput()
	out, err := w.def.Handler.Plan(ctx, w.h.env(""), in)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if len(out.Diff) != 0 || out.StateHash == "" {
		t.Errorf("plan diff %+v hash %q, want an empty diff and a state hash", out.Diff, out.StateHash)
	}
	raw, err := w.apply(t, "01J00000000000000000000001", in)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	var written PolicyWritten
	if err := json.Unmarshal(raw, &written); err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if text, etag := w.srv.Policy(); written.ETag != etag || text != ppPolicy {
		t.Errorf("apply answered etag %q; the fake holds etag %q and the original text %v, want the live ETag and the text kept",
			written.ETag, etag, text == ppPolicy)
	}
	if n := len(w.requests(http.MethodPost, ppValidate)) + len(w.requests(http.MethodPost, ppACL)); n != 0 {
		t.Errorf("%d validate or write requests, want none", n)
	}
	if n := len(w.h.journal.effects()); n != 0 {
		t.Errorf("%d effects journaled, want none", n)
	}
	if err := w.def.Handler.Verify(ctx, w.h.env("01J00000000000000000000001"), in, raw); err != nil {
		t.Errorf("verify after the no-op: %v", err)
	}
}

// TestExPatchWithNoEditsCompletesThroughTheEngine: the engine accepts the
// empty edits list against the action's input schema and completes the change
// with nothing written.
func TestExPatchWithNoEditsCompletesThroughTheEngine(t *testing.T) {
	srv := fake.New(t)
	srv.SetPolicy(ppPolicy)
	h := newTNHost(t)
	acConnect(t, h, srv)
	reg, err := core.NewRegistry(dvModule{[]core.ActionDef{tnWith(srv).policyPatch()}})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	eng, err := lifecycle.New(lifecycle.Config{
		Side: core.SideController, Registry: reg, Validator: schema.NewValidator(), Journal: &coretest.MemJournal{}, Redact: schema.Redact,
		Base: core.Env{DB: h.db, Sealer: coretest.Sealer{}, Clock: h.clock, Log: slog.New(slog.NewTextHandler(io.Discard, nil))},
	})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	t.Cleanup(eng.Close)
	ctx := context.Background()
	pv, err := eng.Plan(ctx, dvAdmin, core.ActionRef{Name: "tailnet.policy.patch", Version: 1}, ppInput(), core.PlanOptions{})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if len(pv.Diff) != 0 {
		t.Errorf("plan diff %+v, want it empty", pv.Diff)
	}
	cv, err := eng.Apply(ctx, dvAdmin, pv.PlanID, core.ApplyOptions{})
	if err != nil || cv.State != core.StateCompleted {
		t.Fatalf("apply = %v, %v (%+v), want a completed change", cv.State, err, cv.Error)
	}
	for _, r := range srv.Cloud().Requests() {
		if r.Method == http.MethodPost && (r.Path == ppACL || r.Path == ppValidate) {
			t.Errorf("provider request %s %s during an empty patch", r.Method, r.Path)
		}
	}
}
```

### Task 5: tailscale: bound the latency probe URL

**Files:**
- Modify: `internal/modules/tailscale/latency.go` (`latencyProbeURLs` and the imports; one new unexported helper beside it)
- Test: `internal/modules/tailscale/latency_test.go` (extend `TestLtProbeURLs`)
- Read only: `docs/dforge/notes/v1-w4-final-review-followups.md`, section "Security finding parked during execution" (the finding and the fix it describes; the note itself is not edited)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `tslocal.DERPRegion` and `tslocal.DERPNode` (`internal/platform/tslocal/derp.go`), unchanged.
- Produces: Contracts "Latency probe URL (from Task 5)". `latencyProbeURLs(r *tslocal.DERPRegion) []string` keeps its signature, its order (node order) and every behaviour it has today (skips a nil node, a `STUNOnly` node, a negative `DERPPort`, an empty `HostName`; leaves port 443 and port 0 out of the URL; brackets an IPv6 literal). It additionally skips a node whose `HostName` is not a bare host name or IP literal, and a node whose `DERPPort` is above 65535.

**Items:** 300

**Depends on:** none
**Implementer:** dforge:impl-sonnet-medium
**Evaluation:** files 1 - spec 2 - coupling 0 - risk 1 = 4

- [ ] **Step 1: Extend the test `internal/modules/tailscale/latency_test.go::TestLtProbeURLs`**

Add these four rows to the `tests` slice of `TestLtProbeURLs`, after its last row (`{"no node", nil, nil}`). The loop that follows the slice is unchanged. `ltURL(host)` is the existing helper that returns `https://<host>/derp/latency-check`. On the current tree the first, second and fourth rows fail because `latencyProbeURLs` builds a URL from any `HostName` and any port; the third row pins behaviour that already holds and must keep holding.

```go
		{"a host name that changes the meaning of the URL is skipped", []*tslocal.DERPNode{
			{Name: "1a", HostName: "a.example.net/admin"},
			{Name: "1b", HostName: "a.example.net?x=1"},
			{Name: "1c", HostName: "a.example.net#x"},
			{Name: "1d", HostName: "user@a.example.net"},
			{Name: "1e", HostName: `a.example.net\x`},
			{Name: "1f", HostName: "a.example .net"},
			{Name: "1g", HostName: "a.example.net\t"},
			{Name: "1h", HostName: "a.example.net\n"},
			{Name: "1i", HostName: "a.example\x00.net"},
			{Name: "1j", HostName: "a.example\x7f.net"},
			{Name: "1k", HostName: "ok.example.net"},
		}, []string{ltURL("ok.example.net")}},
		{"a colon outside an IPv6 literal is skipped", []*tslocal.DERPNode{
			{Name: "1a", HostName: "a.example.net:8080"},
			{Name: "1b", HostName: "10.0.0.1:80"},
			{Name: "1c", HostName: "[2001:db8::1]"},
			{Name: "1d", HostName: "2001:db8::1:zz"},
			{Name: "1e", HostName: "ok.example.net"},
		}, []string{ltURL("ok.example.net")}},
		{"IP literals: IPv6 in brackets, a port after the brackets", []*tslocal.DERPNode{
			{Name: "1a", HostName: "192.0.2.1", DERPPort: 8443},
			{Name: "1b", HostName: "2001:db8::1"},
			{Name: "1c", HostName: "2001:db8::2", DERPPort: 8443},
		}, []string{
			"https://192.0.2.1:8443/derp/latency-check",
			"https://[2001:db8::1]/derp/latency-check",
			"https://[2001:db8::2]:8443/derp/latency-check",
		}},
		{"a port above 65535 is skipped and 65535 is kept", []*tslocal.DERPNode{
			{Name: "1a", HostName: "a.example.net", DERPPort: 65536},
			{Name: "1b", HostName: "b.example.net", DERPPort: 70000},
			{Name: "1c", HostName: "c.example.net", DERPPort: 65535},
			{Name: "1d", HostName: "d.example.net", DERPPort: 443},
		}, []string{"https://c.example.net:65535/derp/latency-check", ltURL("d.example.net")}},
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Run the test to verify it fails**

Run: `mkdir -p /root/.cache/w5-tmp/5 && export TMPDIR=/root/.cache/w5-tmp/5 GOTOOLCHAIN=local && timeout 300 go test -count=1 -run 'TestLtProbeURLs' ./internal/modules/tailscale/`
Expected: FAIL with `urls [...] want [...]` lines for the first, second and fourth added rows (the got list holds URLs built from `a.example.net/admin`, `a.example.net:8080` and port 65536 or 70000). The third row passes. Quote the output in the report.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

In `latency.go`, change `latencyProbeURLs` only:

1. Besides the skips it has today, skip a node whose `DERPPort` is above 65535. Keep `latencyDefaultDERPPort` (443) and port 0 meaning "no port in the URL".
2. Add the unexported helper `latencyHostValid(host string) bool`, documented in one sentence. It answers true for a string that `netip.ParseAddr` accepts (an IPv4 or IPv6 literal), and otherwise true only when the string holds none of `/`, `?`, `#`, `@`, `\`, `:`, no character for which `unicode.IsSpace` or `unicode.IsControl` is true. A node whose `HostName` fails it is skipped. The colon rule is what keeps `name:port`, `ip:port` and a bracketed `[ip]` out: none of them parses as an address, and a colon is allowed only inside an IPv6 literal.
3. Build each URL with `url.URL{Scheme: "https", Host: host, Path: latencyCheckPath}` and take `String()`, never by concatenating strings. When the port is neither 0 nor 443, `Host` is `net.JoinHostPort(hostName, strconv.Itoa(port))` (which brackets an IPv6 literal); otherwise `Host` is the host name, wrapped in `[` `]` only when it is an IPv6 literal.
4. Add the imports `net`, `net/url`, `strings` and `unicode` that the code needs; `strconv` stays. Do not change any other function, constant or comment in the file; a new comment states only the why (the DERP map comes from the tailnet's policy, so its host names are not trusted).
5. Do not edit `docs/dforge/notes/v1-w4-final-review-followups.md`.

Run: `export TMPDIR=/root/.cache/w5-tmp/5 GOTOOLCHAIN=local && timeout 300 go test -count=1 -run 'TestLt' ./internal/modules/tailscale/`
Expected: PASS (every `TestLt*` test, the rows of `TestLtProbeURLs` included).

Then run, with `TMPDIR` and `GOTOOLCHAIN` as above, and record the real output of each: `timeout 600 go build ./...`; `timeout 300 go vet ./...` (no output); `timeout 1500 go test -count=1 ./...` (every line `ok` or `?`); `timeout 600 go test -race -count=1 ./internal/modules/tailscale/`; `timeout 900 golangci-lint run --allow-serial-runners ./...` (`0 issues.`); `timeout 300 go mod tidy -diff` (no output). Finish with `rm -rf /root/.cache/w5-tmp/5 && go clean -testcache`.

`web/src/modules/derp/types.test.ts`:

```ts
import { expect, test } from "vitest"
import { probeFixture, serversFixture } from "./fixtures"
import {
  DERP_ACTION_VERSION,
  DERP_MODULE,
  derpKey,
  isDerpServers,
  isServerProbe,
  type DerpServers,
  type NodeUse,
  type ProbeResult,
  type ProbeSource,
  type RegionDiagnostics,
  type ServerProbe,
  type ServerStatus,
  type Visibility,
} from "./types"

type Json = Record<string, unknown>

function tyClone<T>(v: T): Json {
  return structuredClone(v) as Json
}

test("the module id and the action version are the derp module's", () => {
  expect(DERP_MODULE).toBe("derp")
  expect(DERP_ACTION_VERSION).toBe(1)
})

test("derpKey names one group of queries, a probe by its host", () => {
  expect(derpKey("servers")).toEqual(["derp", "servers"])
  expect(derpKey("probe")).toEqual(["derp", "probe"])
  expect(derpKey("probe", "01JD0000000000000000000011")).toEqual(["derp", "probe", "01JD0000000000000000000011"])
})

test("isDerpServers accepts the controller's JSON", () => {
  expect(isDerpServers(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(serversFixture)))).toBe(true)
  expect(isDerpServers({ servers: [], availableVersion: "v1.104.1" })).toBe(true)
})

test("isDerpServers refuses what is not a complete answer", () => {
  const server = (over: Json) => ({ ...tyClone(serversFixture.servers[0]), ...over })
  const answer = (servers: unknown[]) => ({ servers, availableVersion: "v1.104.1" })
  const rows: [string, unknown][] = [
    ["null", null],
    ["a string", "servers"],
    ["an array", []],
    ["an empty object", {}],
    ["servers that are null", { servers: null, availableVersion: "v1.104.1" }],
    ["a missing availableVersion", { servers: [] }],
    ["a server that is a string", answer(["derp-1"])],
    ["a server without a hostId", answer([server({ hostId: undefined })])],
    ["a regionId that is a string", answer([server({ regionId: "900" })])],
    ["an unknown health", answer([server({ health: "fine" })])],
    ["a certNotAfter that is a number", answer([server({ certNotAfter: 5 })])],
    ["reasons that are not strings", answer([server({ reasons: [1] })])],
    ["a unitActive that is a string", answer([server({ unitActive: "yes" })])],
    ["a missing error", answer([server({ error: undefined })])],
  ]
  for (const [name, value] of rows) expect(isDerpServers(value), name).toBe(false)
})

test("isServerProbe accepts the controller's JSON, a source without region diagnostics and an uninstalled status", () => {
  expect(isServerProbe(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(probeFixture)))).toBe(true)
  const none = { ...tyClone(probeFixture.server), installed: false, mode: "", hostName: "", certName: "", certNotAfter: null, visible: [] }
  expect(isServerProbe({ ...tyClone(probeFixture), server: none })).toBe(true)
  expect(probeFixture.sources[0]?.region).toBeNull()
})

test("isServerProbe refuses what is not a complete answer", () => {
  const source = tyClone(probeFixture.sources[1])
  const node = tyClone(probeFixture.nodes[0])
  const withSources = (sources: unknown) => ({ ...tyClone(probeFixture), sources })
  const rows: [string, unknown][] = [
    ["null", null],
    ["an empty object", {}],
    ["a hostId that is a number", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), hostId: 1 }],
    ["a missing regionCode", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), regionCode: undefined }],
    ["a server that is a string", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), server: "derp-1" }],
    ["a server visibility without an address", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), server: { ...tyClone(probeFixture.server), visible: [{ visible: true, node: "" }] } }],
    ["a server with tags that are null", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), server: { ...tyClone(probeFixture.server), tags: null } }],
    ["sources that are null", withSources(null)],
    ["a source without tls", withSources([{ ...source, tls: undefined }])],
    ["a tls latencyMs that is a string", withSources([{ ...source, tls: { ok: true, latencyMs: "9", error: "" } }])],
    ["a region without errors", withSources([{ ...source, region: { info: [], warnings: [] } }])],
    ["a region that is an array", withSources([{ ...source, region: [] }])],
    ["nodes that are null", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), nodes: null }],
    ["a node with observed as a string", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), nodes: [{ ...node, observed: "yes" }] }],
    ["a ready that is a string", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), ready: "yes" }],
    ["warnings that are not strings", { ...tyClone(probeFixture), warnings: [1] }],
  ]
  for (const [name, value] of rows) expect(isServerProbe(value), name).toBe(false)
})

// Vitest strips types, so this test is checked by `pnpm typecheck`: a
// missing, misspelled or mistyped member of the controller's JSON fails the
// compilation of this file.
test("the wire types carry every member the controller sends", () => {
  const result: ProbeResult = { ok: false, latencyMs: 0, error: "connection refused" }
  const region: RegionDiagnostics = { info: ["a"], warnings: ["b"], errors: ["c"] }
  const source: ProbeSource = { source: "controller", name: "controller", tls: result, stun: result, region, error: "" }
  const seen: Visibility = { address: "100.64.0.1", visible: false, node: "" }
  const status: ServerStatus = {
    installed: true, mode: "letsencrypt", hostName: "derp.example.com", verifyClients: false, derperVersion: "v1.104.1", unitActive: false,
    certName: "", certNotAfter: null, availableVersion: "v1.104.1", linkViaTailnet: true, onTailnet: false, tags: [], addresses: [], visible: [seen],
  }
  const node: NodeUse = { name: "n", address: "100.64.0.1", hostId: "", visible: false, preferred: "", observed: false }
  const probe: ServerProbe = { hostId: "h", regionId: 0, regionCode: "", server: status, sources: [source], nodes: [node], ready: false, warnings: ["w"] }
  const servers: DerpServers = {
    servers: [{
      hostId: "h", host: "derp-1", mode: "ip", hostName: "203.0.113.7", regionId: 900, regionCode: "mydc", derperVersion: "v1.104.1",
      certName: "sha256-raw:00", certNotAfter: "2027-10-20T09:00:00Z", unitActive: true, health: "broken", reasons: ["r"], checkedAt: "2026-11-01T08:00:00Z", error: "",
    }],
    availableVersion: "v1.104.1",
  }
  expect(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(probe)) as ServerProbe).toEqual(probe)
  expect(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(servers)) as DerpServers).toEqual(servers)
})
```

`web/src/modules/derp/derp-change.test.tsx`:

```tsx
import { useState } from "react"
import { screen, waitFor, within } from "@testing-library/react"
import userEvent from "@testing-library/user-event"
import { useQuery } from "@tanstack/react-query"
import { expect, test } from "vitest"
import type { Plan } from "@/api/types"
import { renderWithProviders, stubFetch, type StubHandler } from "@/test/utils"
import { derpKey } from "./types"
import { DerpChange } from "./derp-change"

const DC_HOST = "01JD0000000000000000000011"
const DC_PLAN = "POST /api/v1/actions/derp.server.upgrade/v1/plan"
const DC_APPLY = "POST /api/v1/actions/derp.server.upgrade/v1/apply"
const DC_INPUT = { hostId: DC_HOST }

function dcPlan(over: Partial<Plan> = {}): Plan {
  return {
    planId: "p1", hostId: null, action: "derp.server.upgrade", version: 1, inputHash: "h",
    diff: [{ op: "~", text: "upgrade derper on derp-1 from v1.102.0 to v1.104.1" }], guards: [], expiresAt: "2026-11-01T09:00:00Z", confirmation: null, ...over,
  }
}

const dcApplied = { id: "c1", hostId: null, state: "completed", class: "compensable", error: null, output: null, revertDeadline: null }

function DcHost({ bump }: { bump: (name: string) => number }) {
  const [input, setInput] = useState<Record<string, unknown> | null>(null)
  useQuery({ queryKey: derpKey("servers"), queryFn: () => Promise.resolve(bump("servers")) })
  useQuery({ queryKey: derpKey("probe", DC_HOST), queryFn: () => Promise.resolve(bump("probe")) })
  useQuery({ queryKey: ["tailnet", "policy"], queryFn: () => Promise.resolve(bump("policy")) })
  useQuery({ queryKey: ["resource", "h1", "docker.engine"], queryFn: () => Promise.resolve(bump("engine")) })
  return (
    <>
      <button onClick={() => setInput(DC_INPUT)}>Open</button>
      <DerpChange action="derp.server.upgrade" title="Upgrade the DERP server" input={input} onClose={() => setInput(null)} />
    </>
  )
}

function dcRender(routes: Record<string, StubHandler>) {
  const calls = stubFetch({ "GET /api/v1/changes/c1": { body: { ...dcApplied, output: null } }, ...routes })
  const counts: Record<string, number> = {}
  const bump = (name: string) => (counts[name] = (counts[name] ?? 0) + 1)
  renderWithProviders(<DcHost bump={bump} />)
  return { calls, counts }
}

test("a null input opens no dialog and plans nothing", async () => {
  const { calls } = dcRender({ [DC_PLAN]: { body: dcPlan() } })
  await waitFor(() => expect(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Open" })).toBeTruthy())
  expect(screen.queryByRole("dialog")).toBeNull()
  expect(calls.filter((c) => c.method === "POST")).toHaveLength(0)
})

test("the dialog plans the action on the controller at version 1, without a host or an availability check", async () => {
  const { calls } = dcRender({ [DC_PLAN]: { body: dcPlan() } })
  await userEvent.setup().click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Open" }))
  const dialog = await screen.findByRole("dialog", { name: "Upgrade the DERP server" })
  expect(within(dialog).getByText("derp.server.upgrade/v1 on the controller")).toBeTruthy()
  expect((await within(dialog).findByLabelText("Planned changes")).textContent).toBe("~ upgrade derper on derp-1 from v1.102.0 to v1.104.1")
  const plans = calls.filter((c) => c.method === "POST")
  expect(plans.map((c) => [c.path, c.body])).toEqual([["/api/v1/actions/derp.server.upgrade/v1/plan", { input: DC_INPUT }]])
  expect(calls.some((c) => c.path.includes("availability"))).toBe(false)
})

test("applying posts the plan without a revert timeout", async () => {
  const { calls } = dcRender({ [DC_PLAN]: { body: dcPlan() }, [DC_APPLY]: { body: dcApplied } })
  const user = userEvent.setup()
  await user.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Open" }))
  const dialog = await screen.findByRole("dialog", { name: "Upgrade the DERP server" })
  const apply = await within(dialog).findByRole("button", { name: "Apply" })
  await waitFor(() => expect(apply.hasAttribute("disabled")).toBe(false))
  await user.click(apply)
  await waitFor(() => expect(calls.some((c) => c.path === "/api/v1/actions/derp.server.upgrade/v1/apply")).toBe(true))
  expect(calls.find((c) => c.path.endsWith("/apply"))?.body).toEqual({ planId: "p1" })
  expect(within(dialog).queryByLabelText("Revert after (seconds)")).toBeNull()
})

test("closing the dialog refetches the derp queries and no others", async () => {
  const { counts } = dcRender({ [DC_PLAN]: { body: dcPlan() } })
  const user = userEvent.setup()
  await waitFor(() => expect(counts).toEqual({ servers: 1, probe: 1, policy: 1, engine: 1 }))
  await user.click(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Open" }))
  await screen.findByRole("dialog", { name: "Upgrade the DERP server" })
  expect(counts).toEqual({ servers: 1, probe: 1, policy: 1, engine: 1 })
  await user.keyboard("{Escape}")
  await waitFor(() => expect(screen.queryByRole("dialog")).toBeNull())
  await waitFor(() => expect(counts.servers).toBe(2))
  await waitFor(() => expect(counts.probe).toBe(2))
  expect([counts.policy, counts.engine]).toEqual([1, 1])
})
```

```go
package server

import (
	"crypto/ecdsa"
	"crypto/elliptic"
	"crypto/rand"
	"crypto/x509"
	"crypto/x509/pkix"
	"encoding/pem"

	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/core/errcode"
	"github.com/darkraise/darkpanel/internal/modules/firewall/model"
)

const (
	svHostA = "01JD0000000000000000000011"
	svHostB = "01JD0000000000000000000012"
)

// svPaths is the field paths of a request.invalid_input refusal; it fails the test for any other error.
func svPaths(t *testing.T, err error) []string {
	t.Helper()
	var e *errcode.Error
	if !errors.As(err, &e) || e.Code != errcode.InvalidInput {
		t.Fatalf("error %v, want an *errcode.Error with code request.invalid_input", err)
	}
	var paths []string
	for _, f := range e.Fields {
		paths = append(paths, f.Path)
	}
	return paths
}

func TestSvCheckConfig(t *testing.T) {
	label63 := strings.Repeat("a", 63)
	name253 := label63 + "." + label63 + "." + label63 + "." + strings.Repeat("a", 61)
	tests := []struct {
		name string
		cfg  Config
		want []string // the refused field paths; nil means accepted
	}{
		{"letsencrypt with a DNS name", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp.example.com"}, nil},
		{"letsencrypt with a punycode name and digits", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "xn--bcher-kva-1.example"}, nil},
		{"letsencrypt with a name of 253 bytes", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: name253}, nil},
		{"letsencrypt, verification off", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp.example.com", VerifyClients: false}, nil},
		{"letsencrypt with a name of 254 bytes", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: name253 + "a"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with a label of 64 bytes", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: strings.Repeat("a", 64) + ".com"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with one label", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "localhost"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with an upper-case name", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "Derp.example.com"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with an empty label", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp..example.com"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with a trailing dot", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp.example.com."}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with an underscore", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp_1.example.com"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with a leading hyphen", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "-derp.example.com"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with a trailing hyphen in a label", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp-.example.com"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with an IPv4 literal", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "203.0.113.7"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with an IPv6 literal", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "2001:db8::1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"letsencrypt with an empty name", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a public IPv4 address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "203.0.113.7"}, nil},
		{"ip with a public IPv6 address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "2001:db8::1"}, nil},
		{"ip with a private address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "10.0.0.5"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a private 192.168 address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "192.168.1.1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a loopback address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "127.0.0.1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with the IPv6 loopback", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "::1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a link-local address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "169.254.1.1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with an IPv6 link-local address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "fe80::1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with the unspecified address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "0.0.0.0"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a multicast address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "224.0.0.1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a Tailscale IPv4 address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "100.64.0.9"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a Tailscale IPv6 address", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "fd7a:115c:a1e0::1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with an upper-case IPv6 literal", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "2001:DB8::1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with an IPv6 literal that is not shortened", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "2001:db8:0:0:0:0:0:1"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a prefix length", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "203.0.113.7/32"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a space", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "203.0.113.7 "}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with a DNS name", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "derp.example.com"}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"ip with an empty address", Config{Mode: ModeIP}, []string{"/hostName"}},
		{"an unknown mode", Config{Mode: "manual", HostName: "derp.example.com"}, []string{"/mode"}},
		{"an empty mode", Config{HostName: "derp.example.com"}, []string{"/mode"}},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		err := CheckConfig(tt.cfg)
		if tt.want == nil {
			if err != nil {
				t.Errorf("%s: CheckConfig = %v, want nil", tt.name, err)
			}
			continue
		}
		if err == nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: CheckConfig = nil, want a refusal of %v", tt.name, tt.want)
			continue
		}
		if got := svPaths(t, err); !slices.Equal(got, tt.want) {
			t.Errorf("%s: refused fields %v, want %v", tt.name, got, tt.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestSvFlags(t *testing.T) {

		want []string
	}{
		{"ip mode verifies clients", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "203.0.113.7", VerifyClients: true},
			append(slices.Clone(common), "-hostname", "203.0.113.7", "-certmode", "manual", "-certdir", "/var/lib/darkpanel/derp/certs", "-verify-clients")},
		{"letsencrypt without verification", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp.example.com"},
			append(slices.Clone(common), "-hostname", "derp.example.com", "-certmode", "letsencrypt", "-certdir", "/var/lib/darkpanel/derp/certs")},
	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		if got := Flags(tt.cfg); !slices.Equal(got, tt.want) {
			t.Errorf("%s: Flags =\n%q\nwant\n%q", tt.name, got, tt.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestSvUnitFile(t *testing.T) {
	const head = "[Unit]\n" +
		"Description=Darkpanel DERP server\n" +
		"After=network-online.target tailscaled.service\n" +
		"Wants=network-online.target\n" +
		"\n" +
		"[Service]\n" +
		"User=darkpanel-derp\n" +

	const tail = "\n" +
		"AmbientCapabilities=CAP_NET_BIND_SERVICE\n" +

		"NoNewPrivileges=yes\n" +
		"Restart=on-failure\n" +
		"RestartSec=5\n" +
		"\n" +
		"[Install]\n" +
		"WantedBy=multi-user.target\n"
	tests := []struct {
		name string
		cfg  Config
		want string
	}{
		{"ip mode with client verification", Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "203.0.113.7", VerifyClients: true},
			head + "-hostname 203.0.113.7 -certmode manual -certdir /var/lib/darkpanel/derp/certs -verify-clients" + tail},
		{"letsencrypt without client verification", Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp.example.com"},

	}
	for _, tt := range tests {
		if got := string(UnitFile(tt.cfg)); got != tt.want {
			t.Errorf("%s: UnitFile =\n%s\nwant\n%s", tt.name, got, tt.want)
		}
	}
}

func TestSvCertFiles(t *testing.T) {
	crt, key, ok := CertFiles(Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "203.0.113.7"})
	if crt != "203.0.113.7.crt" || key != "203.0.113.7.key" || !ok {
		t.Errorf("CertFiles(ip) = %q, %q, %v; want 203.0.113.7.crt, 203.0.113.7.key, true", crt, key, ok)
	}
	crt, key, ok = CertFiles(Config{Mode: ModeIP, HostName: "2001:db8::1"})
	if crt != "2001:db8::1.crt" || key != "2001:db8::1.key" || !ok {
		t.Errorf("CertFiles(ip6) = %q, %q, %v; want 2001:db8::1.crt, 2001:db8::1.key, true", crt, key, ok)
	}
	crt, key, ok = CertFiles(Config{Mode: ModeLetsEncrypt, HostName: "derp.example.com"})
	if crt != "" || key != "" || ok {
		t.Errorf("CertFiles(letsencrypt) = %q, %q, %v; want empty names and false", crt, key, ok)
	}
}

func TestSvPin(t *testing.T) {
	// SHA-256 of "abc", the FIPS 180 test vector.
	if got, want := Pin([]byte("abc")), "sha256-raw:ba7816bf8f01cfea414140de5dae2223b00361a396177a9cb410ff61f20015ad"; got != want {
		t.Errorf("Pin(abc) = %q, want %q", got, want)
	}
	if a, b := Pin([]byte("a")), Pin([]byte("b")); a == b {
		t.Errorf("Pin of different inputs is %q for both", a)
	}
}

func svCert(t *testing.T, notAfter time.Time) []byte {
	t.Helper()
	key, err := ecdsa.GenerateKey(elliptic.P256(), rand.Reader)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	tpl := &x509.Certificate{
		SerialNumber: big.NewInt(1),
		Subject:      pkix.Name{CommonName: "derpkey"},
		NotBefore:    notAfter.Add(-365 * 24 * time.Hour),
		NotAfter:     notAfter,
	}
	der, err := x509.CreateCertificate(rand.Reader, tpl, tpl, &key.PublicKey, key)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return der
}

func TestSvParseCert(t *testing.T) {
	notAfter := time.Date(2027, 10, 20, 9, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
	der := svCert(t, notAfter)
	certPEM := pem.EncodeToMemory(&pem.Block{Type: "CERTIFICATE", Bytes: der})
	other := svCert(t, notAfter.Add(time.Hour))
	other = pem.EncodeToMemory(&pem.Block{Type: "CERTIFICATE", Bytes: other})
	params := pem.EncodeToMemory(&pem.Block{Type: "EC PARAMETERS", Bytes: []byte{1, 2, 3}})

	for name, in := range map[string][]byte{
		"a certificate": certPEM,
		"a certificate after a block of another type": append(slices.Clone(params), certPEM...),
		"the first of two certificates":               append(slices.Clone(certPEM), other...),
	} {
		gotDER, gotAfter, err := ParseCert(in)
		if err != nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: ParseCert: %v", name, err)
			continue
		}
		if !reflect.DeepEqual(gotDER, der) {
			t.Errorf("%s: DER differs from the first certificate's", name)
		}
		if !gotAfter.Equal(notAfter) {
			t.Errorf("%s: NotAfter = %v, want %v", name, gotAfter, notAfter)
		}
	}

	for name, in := range map[string][]byte{
		"empty input":             nil,
		"text":                    []byte("not a pem file"),
		"only another block type": params,
		"a certificate block that is not a certificate": pem.EncodeToMemory(&pem.Block{Type: "CERTIFICATE", Bytes: []byte("nope")}),
	} {
		_, _, err := ParseCert(in)
		if err == nil {
			t.Errorf("%s: ParseCert = nil, want an error", name)
			continue
		}
		if !strings.HasPrefix(err.Error(), "server:") {
			t.Errorf("%s: error %q does not start with the package name", name, err)
		}
	}
}

func TestSvRuleID(t *testing.T) {
	golden := map[[2]string]string{
		{svHostA, "80/tcp"}:   "052TM0KFCBMRCRHPQNF8ENDQCB",
		{svHostA, "443/tcp"}:  "0WKF7EEXYG37R9V2YAMNJ3FT3E",
		{svHostA, "3478/udp"}: "0PDBWS9M0AGNGD1BH1SB6APK0D",
		{svHostB, "80/tcp"}:   "0G9DBB9VCFZ8CXS7N9QCXBTR36",
		{svHostB, "443/tcp"}:  "0PF3SB818V8X9DV0J1V0HRF6PQ",
		{svHostB, "3478/udp"}: "0XY1KXY2CSQ55CBR3SKJ1QPDBT",
	}
	ulid := regexp.MustCompile(`^[0-9A-HJKMNP-TV-Z]{26}$`)
	seen := map[string]bool{}
	for k, want := range golden {
		got := RuleID(k[0], k[1])
		if got != want {
			t.Errorf("RuleID(%s, %s) = %s, want %s", k[0], k[1], got, want)
		}
		if !ulid.MatchString(got) {
			t.Errorf("RuleID(%s, %s) = %s does not match the firewall's ULID pattern", k[0], k[1], got)
		}
		seen[got] = true
	}
	if len(seen) != len(golden) {
		t.Errorf("%d distinct ids for %d host and port pairs", len(seen), len(golden))
	}
}

func TestSvPortRules(t *testing.T) {
	rule := func(id string, proto model.Proto, port string) model.Rule {
		return model.Rule{
			ID: id, Action: model.ActionAllow, Direction: model.DirIn, Proto: proto,
			From: model.Any, To: model.Any, ToPort: port, Family: model.FamilyBoth,
		}
	}
	want := []model.Rule{
		rule("052TM0KFCBMRCRHPQNF8ENDQCB", model.ProtoTCP, "80"),
		rule("0WKF7EEXYG37R9V2YAMNJ3FT3E", model.ProtoTCP, "443"),
		rule("0PDBWS9M0AGNGD1BH1SB6APK0D", model.ProtoUDP, "3478"),
	}
	got := PortRules(svHostA)
	if !reflect.DeepEqual(got, want) {
		t.Errorf("PortRules =\n%+v\nwant\n%+v", got, want)
	}
	for _, r := range got {
		if err := r.Validate(); err != nil {
			t.Errorf("rule %s is outside the firewall grammar: %v", r.ID, err)
		}
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

Create `server.go` with the package comment `// Package server is the DERP server's pure logic (spec 4.3): the unit file, derper's flags, the certificate pin and the firewall rules. It does no I/O.` Declare the constants of Contracts "DERP server unit" with their stated values and `Config` as written there, then:

1. `CheckConfig(c Config) error`. A refusal is `*errcode.Error` with `errcode.InvalidInput`, a client-safe `Detail` ("the DERP server configuration is invalid") and `Fields` holding one `errcode.FieldError{Path, Message}` per failing field. A mode that is neither constant gives only `/mode` (the host name cannot be judged without a mode). For `letsencrypt`, `/hostName` fails when the name parses as an IP address, is longer than 253 bytes, has fewer than two labels, or has a label that is not 1 to 63 characters of `a-z`, `0-9` and `-` not starting or ending with `-` (an upper-case letter, an empty label, a trailing dot and an underscore all fail this rule). For `ip`, `/hostName` fails unless `netip.ParseAddr` accepts it, it has no zone, `Addr.String()` equals the input (canonical form), `IsGlobalUnicast` is true, `IsPrivate` is false, and `link.IsTailscaleAddr` (package `internal/modules/tailscale/link`) is false; loopback, link-local, multicast and unspecified addresses already fail `IsGlobalUnicast`. `VerifyClients` is not checked.
2. `Flags(c Config) []string` returns one element per token in the order of the contract: `-c`, `KeyPath`, `-a`, `:443`, `-http-port`, `80`, `-stun`, `-stun-port`, `3478`, `-hostname`, `HostName`, `-certmode`, `letsencrypt` for `ModeLetsEncrypt` or `manual` for `ModeIP`, `-certdir`, `CertDir`, then `-verify-clients` only when `VerifyClients`. Build the port values from the constants (`strconv.Itoa`), never from a second literal. It does not call `CheckConfig`.
3. `UnitFile(c Config) []byte` returns the text of Contracts "DERP unit text" with `Flags(c)` joined by single spaces after the program path, every line ending in `\n` and no other trailing text. Build `ExecStart` from `BinaryPath` and `ServiceUser` from the constant.
4. `CertFiles(c Config)`: for `ModeIP` the names `HostName + ".crt"` and `HostName + ".key"` and true; otherwise two empty strings and false.
5. `Pin(der []byte) string`: `PinPrefix` plus the lower-case hex SHA-256 of `der`.
6. `ParseCert(pemBytes []byte) (der []byte, notAfter time.Time, err error)`: walk `pem.Decode` blocks until the first of type `CERTIFICATE`; return its bytes and the `NotAfter` that `x509.ParseCertificate` reads from them, in UTC. No `CERTIFICATE` block, or bytes that do not parse, is an error whose text starts `server:`. The error text never includes the input.
7. `RuleID(hostID, port string) string`: SHA-256 of `"darkpanel/derp/" + hostID + "/" + port`, encoded in Crockford base32 with the alphabet `0123456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTVWXYZ` and no padding (5 bits per character, most significant first), the first 25 characters, prefixed with `"0"`.
8. `PortRules(hostID string) []model.Rule` returns the three rules of the contract in the order `80/tcp`, `443/tcp`, `3478/udp`, each `model.Rule{ID: RuleID(hostID, port), Action: model.ActionAllow, Direction: model.DirIn, Proto: tcp or udp, From: model.Any, To: model.Any, ToPort: "80" / "443" / "3478" (from the port constants), Family: model.FamilyBoth}` with no comment and no other field set.

Every exported identifier has a doc comment; comments elsewhere only for a non-obvious why. The package imports no `os`, `net` dial or file API.
